.

•

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
is the codiscrete functor—right adjoint to the discrete
functor—and sends a cubical space
X
to the cubical space whose value at
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}

is given by
X\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{2^{n}}
.

•

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
sends a cubical space
X
to
X\circ\mathrm{op}
where

\mathrm{op}
is the functor on the cube category reversing
0
and
1
.

If we consider a category
C\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
, then these interpretations of
\flat
and
\mathrm{op}

have very concrete meanings:
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
is the underlying discrete groupoid of objects
of
C
and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
is the opposite category. In general
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\sharp\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\sharp\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sharp\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sharp\mid C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
will fail to be
a category even when
C
is—its utility is mostly in forcing
\flat
to be a left adjoint. We note
that the first two modalities are an instance of cohesion
(
Lawvere 2007
;
Shulman 2018
;
Myers and Riley 2023
)
.

Note, however, that these are presently just intuitions from a particular model. To actually make
these have force within
TT
\boxbslash
itself, we require additional axioms governing the behavior of

\mathbb{I}
and its interactions with modalities. A complete list of axioms is given in
Appendix

B

so we discuss only axioms playing a direct role in the proofs given in the paper.

First, we have axioms governing the behavior of
\flat
and
\mathbb{I}
:

{axiom}
[]

0,1:\mathbb{I}
induce an equivalence
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\simeq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

{axiom}
[]
If
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
, then
\mathsf{isEquiv}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
if

\mathsf{isEquiv}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to A^{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to A^{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to A^{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to A^{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Intuitively, the first of this pair states that
\mathbb{I}
has just two global elements: the specified
endpoints
0
and
1
. The second axiom ensures that
\mathbb{I}
“detects” whether a type is of the
form
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
. Phrased pithily: a type
A
is equivalent to a groupoid of objects if and
only if it has no non-invertible synthetic morphisms. These axioms—particularly the last
one—are what force
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
to match our earlier idea of taking the groupoid core.

The next axiom forces cubes
\mathbb{I}^{n}
to form a
separating family
among all types. That is, to
detect whether a particular (
\flat
-)map is invertible, it suffices to check whether post-composing
with this map induces equivalences between groupoids of maps
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
:

{axiom}
[]

If
A,B\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
and
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}A\to B
, then
f
is an equivalence if

\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}\mathsf{isEquiv}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\dagger}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to B$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to Since
\boxbslash
has a universal “mapping-out” property, we have a succinct description of

\boxbslash A\to B
in terms of
A\to B
. In general, there is no such simple relationship between

A\to\boxbslash B
and
A\to B
, with the notable exception of the case when
A=\Delta^{n}
.
Intuitively,
\boxbslash B
ensures that all hyper-cubes in
B
are determined uniquely by the composite
of simplices and so while the hyper-cubes of
\boxbslash B
may be quite different than in
B
, the
collection of
simplices
in
B
remains unchanged. The following records this relationship:

{axiom}
[]

For every
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
, the following holds:

\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{n:{\mathsf{Nat}}}}\mathsf{isEquiv}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\dagger}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to Our final axiom in this section records the right adjoint to
\mathbb{I}\to-
mentioned in

Section

1
. We must be somewhat more careful in stating this than

, as we
wish to ensure that the postulated adjoint is fully coherent, so we use the following formulation
which records that the adjoint exists uniquely:

{axiom}
[]

For every
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
, the following holds:

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{\mathbb{I}}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U},\epsilon\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A_{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid B\to A_{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid B\to A_{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid B\to A_{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid B\to A_{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid B\to A_{\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid B^{\mathbb{I}}\to A$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Intuitively,
A_{\mathbb{I}}
is the application of this “amazing” right adjoint to a

\flat
-annotated type
A
and
\epsilon
is the counit of this adjunction at
A
. From this data we
may reconstruct a functor
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\mathbb{I}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
.
Crucially, this axiom only applies when
A
is
\flat
-annotated;
Licata et al. 2018
show that
requiring it for arbitrary types forces
\mathbb{I}=\mathbf{1}
, contradicting

0\neq 1:\mathbb{I}
. Moreover, as noted earlier, this axiom is
not
validated by the
model of
STT
in simplicial spaces; it is only after shifting to cubical spaces that

Section

2.3
is valid. Concretely, it is often the case that even if
A
is known
to be simplicial, the same will not be true of
A_{\mathbb{I}}
. Our main use of this axiom is to “transpose” various

\flat
-annotated predicates
X^{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathsf{HProp}
into predicates
X\to\mathsf{HProp}
. For convenience, we
bundle up this process into the following lemma:

Lemma 2.6.

If
\phi\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}\to\mathsf{HProp}
, there is a

\bar{\phi}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}\to\mathsf{HProp}
equipped with a canonical equivalence:

\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\prod_{A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\bar{\phi}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\bar{\phi}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\bar{\phi}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\bar{\phi}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\simeq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:X^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

Proof.

Fix a predicate
\phi\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathsf{HProp}
(for simplicity, we handle only the case of

n=1
as the case general case is identical modulo notational clutter). We begin by using

Section

2.3
to obtain a map
\phi_{\mathbb{I}}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}\to\mathsf{HProp}_{\mathbb{I}}

where
\mathsf{HProp}_{\mathbb{I}}
is the unique type arising from applying the amazing right adjoint to
\mathsf{HProp}
.
Next, following
Gratzer et al. 2024
, we observe that the tautological family

\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathsf{HProp}_{\mathbb{I}}
is classified by a map
\mathsf{HProp}_{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathsf{HProp}
(in particular, it
is a small family) and, composing this with
\phi_{\mathbb{I}}
, we obtain our desired
\bar{\phi}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathsf{HProp}
. In total, we have the following diagram:

\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{I}}
\mathsf{HProp}_{\mathbb{I}}
\mathbf{1}
\mathsf{HProp}
\mathcal{U}
\phi_{\mathbb{I}}
\bar{\phi}

To show that the desired equivalence holds, fix
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}
. We wish to show
that
\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}\bar{\phi}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
holds if and only if

\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}}}}\bar{\phi}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. The former holds if and only if there is a
(necessarily unique) extension of the above diagram:

\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{I}}
\mathsf{HProp}_{\mathbb{I}}
\mathbf{1}
\mathsf{HProp}
\mathcal{U}
\phi_{\mathbb{I}}
X
A

After transposing
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\mathbb{I}}
(and discarding the right-hand vertical map as it is redundant
for our purposes), we see that the left-hand triangle of this diagram is precisely equivalent to
the following:

\mathbf{1}
\mathsf{HProp}{}
\mathcal{U}^{\mathbb{I}}
\phi
X^{\mathbb{I}}
A_{*}

Examining this diagram yields the desired conclusion.
∎

Finally,
Gratzer et al. 2024
have shown that
TT
\boxbslash
(
MTT
with this mode theory
extended with all of the above axioms) has a model in cubical spaces. They further show, based on a
result of
Riehl and Shulman 2017
, that categories in
TT
\boxbslash
are realized by a standard model of

\infty
-categories: complete Segal spaces
(
Rezk 2001
)
.

Theorem 2.7.

There is a model of
TT
\boxbslash
in cubical spaces
\mathbf{PSh}_{\mathbf{sSet}}(\square)
. In this model, categories
are realized by complete Segal spaces.

2.4.
Category theory in triangulated type theory

We require some of the category theory developed previously in
TT
\boxbslash
and

STT

(
Riehl and Shulman 2017
;
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
;
Gratzer et al. 2024
;
Gratzer et al. 2025
)
. To keep this paper more
self-contained, we recall the relevant results and definitions here.

As noted by
Riehl and Shulman 2017
, a natural transformation between functors
f,g:C\to D
—i.e., an
element of
\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}
—corresponds precisely to a family
\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.
Consequently, a pointwise invertible natural transformation is invertible. We note a refinement of
this statement by
Gratzer et al. 2025
which further reduces this to
\flat
elements (i.e., objects) of

C
:
3
3

3

Gratzer et al. 2025
prove

Lemmas

2.8
and
2.10
using the twisted arrow
modality, which we have chosen not to include in
TT
\boxbslash
for simplicity. More elementary proofs
merely relying on
Section

2.3
are possible and so there is no issue with their use in

TT
\boxbslash
.

Lemma 2.8.

If
C,D\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
are categories and

f,g\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to D
, then a natural transformation
\alpha\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,g$}\mathclose{}{)}}

is invertible if and only if for all
c\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C
the map
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\,c,g\,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is invertible.

We similarly have a synthetic version of the classical result that full, faithful and essentially
surjective functors are equivalences.

Lemma 2.9.

If
C,D\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{isCat}}
, then
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to D
is invertible
iff
f
is essentially surjective and fully faithful on
\flat
-elements of
C
.

Our calculations with cocartesian fibrations in
Sections

3
and
4
will rely on the
theory of adjunctions in
TT
\boxbslash
. To begin with, an adjunction between two functors
f:C\to D
and

g:D\to C
is given by a collection of equivalences:

\alpha:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{d:D}}{\textstyle\prod_{d:D}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{d:D}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{d:D}}\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\,c,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\,c,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\,c,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\,c,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}\simeq\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c,g\,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c,g\,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c,g\,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c,g\,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Note that we do not require any additional naturality constraints on
\alpha
, these are
automatically enforced by virtue of working synthetically. We say
f
is a left adjoint if there exists
a (necessarily unique)
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle g,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle g,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle g,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle g,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, and dually that
g
is a right adjoint if there exists

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. It is often difficult to construct such a family of equivalences directly, so we
often use the following result of
Gratzer et al. 2025
:

Lemma 2.10.

If
C,D\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
are categories, then
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to D
is a left adjoint
iff for all
d\mathrel{:_{\flat}}D
there exists
c\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C
and

\epsilon\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}
such that the following is an equivalence for all

c^{\prime}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C
:

\epsilon_{*}\circ f:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c^{\prime},c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c^{\prime},c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c^{\prime},c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c^{\prime},c$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We shall also have use for the various concrete examples of categories constructed by

Gratzer et al. 2024
. Foremost among these is the category of groupoids
{\mathcal{S}}
—the

\infty
-categorical analog of the category of sets. Like our eventual definition of the
category of categories, this is characterized through a universal property.

Definition 2.11.

A family of types
A:X\to\mathcal{U}
is
covariant
if it is right orthogonal to the inclusion

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to\mathbb{I}
.

More intuitively, covariant families are families of groupoids such that synthetic homomorphisms of the
base lift coherently to functors of the fibers. We shall give more exposition of this idea indirectly
in
Section

3
when we study their generalization: cocartesian families. Covariant
families are closed under numerous properties, including precomposition,
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum}{\textstyle\sum}{\scriptstyle\sum}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum}
-types, etc. We
now define
{\mathcal{S}}
as the base of the universal covariant family:

Definition 2.12.

{\mathcal{S}}
is the unique subtype of the universe such that
{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{U}
is covariant, and for
all
X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
, the canonical map
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}\mathsf{isCov}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}\mathsf{isCov}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}\mathsf{isCov}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:X\to\mathcal{U}}}\mathsf{isCov}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}
is an equivalence.

Consequently, the type of objects of
{\mathcal{S}}
, i.e.,
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
, is immediately seen to be
equivalent to
\flat
-covariant families over
\mathbf{1}
. These, in turn, are equivalent to

\flat
-groupoids. The main result of
Gratzer et al. 2024
extends this characterization to synthetic
morphisms:

Theorem 2.13.

{\mathcal{S}}
is a category, and there is a canonical equivalence
\hom_{{\mathcal{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}\simeq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Moreover, under this equivalence, identities and composition of synthetic morphisms are
realized by identity functions and ordinary function composition.

Finally, we record a minor but useful result stating that
\mathbb{I}
,
and some types derived from it, form categories
(
Gratzer et al. 2024
)
.

Lemma 2.14.

\mathbb{I}
,
\mathbb{I}^{n}
, and
\Delta^{n}
all form categories.

2.5.
Technical results on iso-inner fibrations

Before moving on to the main focus of this article, we record a few technical results about
iso-inner fibrations and the
\boxbslash
modality.

Definition 2.15.

We write
\mathsf{Spine}^{n}
for the iterated pushout
\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}
which glues together
n
copies of
\mathbb{I}
attaching
0
to
1
and
1
to
0
.

Lemma 2.16.

If
f\mathrel{:}X\to Y
is inner it is orthogonal to

\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
for all
n\geq 2
.

Proof.

The
n=2
case is by definition, so we proceed by induction. By induction hypothesis,
\mathsf{Spine}^{n+1}\to\Delta^{n}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}
is orthogonal to all inner maps(left maps are closed
under pushouts). It suffices to show that
\Delta^{n}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}\to\Delta^{n+1}

is orthogonal to all inner maps. Unfolding these conditionals, we must show the following:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}=1\lor i=0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}=1\lor i=0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}=1\lor i=0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}=1\lor i=0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

This, in
turn, is a retract of
\mathbb{I}^{n-1}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to\mathbb{I}^{n-1}\times\Delta^{2}
.
∎

The converse of this lemma also holds if we further assume that the relevant types are simplicial.
It is an easy corollary of the following result:

Lemma 2.17.

If
X,Y\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
and
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to Y
is

\flat
-orthogonal to
\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
so too is
f^{\mathbb{I}}

Proof.

We must show that
\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}
is
\flat
-orthogonal

f
. To this end, we note the following identification:

\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\exists k\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}.\,v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i\geq v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\exists k\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}.\,v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i\geq v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\exists k\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}.\,v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i\geq v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\exists k\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,\dots,n+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}.\,v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i\geq v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

Here, by convention, we treat
v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}=1
and
v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}=v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0
. In what follows,
we write
\Phi_{k}
for the condition
v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}\mathclose{}{)}}\geq i\geq v\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Consequently, to show the desired lifting it suffices to show that there is a unique such lift for

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Phi_{k_{0}}\times\dots\times\Phi_{k_{l}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\Phi_{k_{0}}\times\dots\times\Phi_{k_{l}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Phi_{k_{0}}\times\dots\times\Phi_{k_{l}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Phi_{k_{0}}\times\dots\times\Phi_{k_{l}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
. A moment’s
thought reveals that each such intersection is a sub-simplex of
\Delta^{n}\times\mathbb{I}
. In
particular, each
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Phi_{k}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\Phi_{k}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Phi_{k}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Phi_{k}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
is
\Delta^{n+1}
and each higher intersection is a
smaller simplex. Moreover, its intersection with
\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}
is either exactly the spine of this smaller
simplex (in the cases of
\Phi_{0}
and
\Phi_{n}
or higher intersections) or

\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\Delta^{2}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots
.
In either case, the unique lifting exists by assumption on
f
(in the latter case, by 2-for-3 and
the closure of left classes under pushouts, as one may see by observing the following
decomposition:

\mathsf{Spine}^{n+1}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\Delta^{2}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\Delta^{2}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\Delta^{2}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\Delta^{2}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\dots\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}\to\Delta^{n+1}
).
∎

Corollary 2.18.

If
X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
is simplicial, it suffices to show that it is
\flat
-orthogonal to

\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
to prove that it is Segal.

If

\boxbslash X

Proof.

To prove this, we argue that

\displaystyle\simeq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

Consequently, we are reduced to the same question for

.

Lemma 2.20.

If
X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
is Segal and Rezk then
\boxbslash X
is Rezk.

Proof.

We must show that
\boxbslash X
is
\flat
-orthogonal to
\mathbb{E}\times\Delta^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
and, since

\boxbslash X
is Segal, we may reduce immediately to the case where
n=0
or
n=1
. The first case
is an immediate consequence of simplicial stability, as
\mathbb{E}
is built by pushing out various
simplices and therefore maps
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{E}\to\boxbslash X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{E}\to\boxbslash X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{E}\to\boxbslash X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{E}\to\boxbslash X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
correspond to those maps which factor
through
X
.

For the
n=1
case, we must show that diagrams of the following shape in
X
are determined by
the bottom-most edge

x_{0}
x_{1}
x_{2}
x_{3}
f
\iota_{1}

\sim

\sim

\iota_{0}
g

By simplicial stability along with the previous case, we may safely assume that each of these
components (including the relevant 2-cells and section-retraction pairs) all come from
X
.
Moreover, since
X
is Segal, the top simplex is redundant. In particular, it is equivalent to the
type

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\iota_{1}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{E}\to X}}{\textstyle\sum_{\iota_{1}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{E}\to X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\iota_{1}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{E}\to X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\iota_{1}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{E}\to X}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\textstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to X}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{p\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\iota\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{p\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\iota\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle .
By the

and replace

by simply

. After this replacement, the

It finally suffices to show that the bottom triangle is equivalent to the bottom edge. However, we
may replace this triangle by the corresponding inner horn, whereafter another application of the

finishes things off.
∎

Corollary 2.21.

If
X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
is Segal and Rezk, then
\boxbslash X
is a category.

3.
Recollections on cocartesian fibrations

Our eventual goal of characterizing
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid X\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
crucially depends on the theory
of
cocartesian families

(
Joyal 2008
;
Lurie 2009
)
. These are a subset of type families
A:X\to\mathcal{U}
for which
(1) each
A\,x
is a category, and (2) each morphism
f:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in
X
induces a transport
function
A\,x\to A\,y
. We shall require that these transport functions are
functorial, and that the coherences enforcing functoriality are themselves coherent, etc.
In order to structure all of this data, we ask for
A
to satisfy a number of propositions
that, when combined, give rise to (1) and (2) above. While somewhat indirect, this
accounts for the infinite hierarchy of coherences that would otherwise be impossible to write
down. This material has been developed within
STT

(
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
)
. We recall it
for the reader’s benefit.

3.1.
The definition of cocartesian families

Consider a family
A:X\to\mathcal{U}
and write
\rho^{A}
for the canonical map given by restriction and
projection

\widetilde{A}^{\Delta^{2}}\to\widetilde{A}^{\Lambda^{2}_{0}}\times_{X^{\Lambda^{2}_{0}}}X^{\Delta^{2}}
.
Note that the restriction
\widetilde{A}^{\Delta^{2}}
to
\widetilde{A}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}

along with the corresponding map

\widetilde{A}^{\Lambda^{2}_{0}}\times_{{\Lambda^{2}_{0}}\to X}X^{\Delta^{2}}\to\widetilde{A}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}

commute with
\rho^{A}
, so we may view
\rho^{A}
as a map between two families of types over

\mathbb{I}\to\widetilde{A}
. Given
x:\mathbb{I}\to\widetilde{A}
, we denote by
\rho^{A}_{x}
the
restriction of
\rho^{A}
to the fibers of these families over
x
.

Definition 3.1.

A morphism
f:\mathbb{I}\to\widetilde{A}
is
cocartesian
in
A

(written
\mathsf{isCocartArr}_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}
) if
\rho^{A}_{f}
is an equivalence.

Informally,
f:\mathbb{I}\to\widetilde{A}
is cocartesian if diagrams of the following shape have a
unique lift whenever
\Delta^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{0}\to\bar{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\to\Delta^{2}\to\widetilde{A}
is
f
:

\Lambda^{2}_{0}
\Delta^{2}
\widetilde{A}
X

Definition 3.2.

A family
A\mathrel{:}X\to\mathcal{U}
is

cocartesian
if it is iso-inner, each
A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is simplicial, and
the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{u\mathrel{:}\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\textstyle\prod_{u\mathrel{:}\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{u\mathrel{:}\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{u\mathrel{:}\mathbb{I}\to X}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{a\mathrel{:}A(u\,0)}}{\textstyle\prod_{a\mathrel{:}A(u\,0)}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{a\mathrel{:}A(u\,0)}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{a\mathrel{:}A(u\,0)}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:}\hom_{u}^{A}(a,\bullet)}}{\textstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:}\hom_{u}^{A}(a,\bullet)}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:}\hom_{u}^{A}(a,\bullet)}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:}\hom_{u}^{A}(a,\bullet)}}\mathsf{isCocartArr}_{A}(f)

This requirement is a proposition
(
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
)
and each fiber of such a family is a
category, satisfying the first of our requirements.

Example 3.3.

For every category
A
the codomain map
{A^{\mathbb{I}}\to A}
is cocartesian. The domain projection is
cocartesian iff
A
has pushouts.

{remark}

An arrow is
vertical
if it maps to an isomorphism. In a cocartesian fibration, every arrow
factors as “vertical
\circ
cocartesian”.

In case that
X
is a category and
A
is simplicial and iso-inner, a slick characterization of
cocartesian families becomes available.

Proposition 3.4 (
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
).

An iso-inner family
A\mathrel{:}X\to\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
over a category
X
is cocartesian iff the map

(\widetilde{A})^{\mathbb{I}}\to(\widetilde{A})^{\{0\}}\times_{X^{\{0\}}}X^{\mathbb{I}}
has a left adjoint
right inverse.
5
5

5

That is, a left adjoint where the unit map is an isomorphism.

This characterization implies many closure properties such as under composition, pullback, and Leibniz
cotensors
(
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
)
.

For the second desideratum of cocartesian families, we define the cocartesian transport
operation, providing the desired functors between fibers. If
A:X\to\mathcal{U}
is cocartesian and

u:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, transport
u_{!}\mathrel{:}A\,x\to A\,y
is defined by mapping

a\mathrel{:}A\,x
to the codomain of the (unique) cocartesian lift of
u
starting at
a
.

Proposition 3.5.

If
A\mathrel{:}X\to\mathcal{U}
is cocartesian, then cocartesian transport is
functorial
,
i.e.,
(vu)_{!}=v_{!}\circ u_{!}
and
(\mathsf{id})_{!}=\mathsf{id}
.

Definition 3.6.

For
A,B\mathrel{:}X\to\mathcal{U}
cocartesian, we say that

f:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}A\,x\to B\,x
is a
cocartesian functor
if
f
preserves cocartesian arrows. We write
A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B
for the type of cocartesian functors.

For our construction of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
, it will be helpful to develop the theory of
locally

cocartesian fibrations. These are families
A:X\to\mathcal{U}
that are cocartesian after
restriction
A\circ f
for every
f:\mathbb{I}\to X
. As setup,
for a family
A:\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}
, we call an edge
a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i

locally cocartesian
if the following proposition holds:

\displaystyle\mathsf{isLocallyCoCart}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{HProp}

We then define the structure of having locally cocartesian lifts:

\displaystyle\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}\,A=\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{a_{0}:A\,0}}{\textstyle\prod_{a_{0}:A\,0}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{a_{0}:A\,0}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{a_{0}:A\,0}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{a:\hom_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{a:\hom_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{a:\hom_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{a:\hom_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a_{0},\bullet$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{isLocallyCoCart}\,a

Unlike for cocartesian edges, locally cocartesian edges need not compose. Let us quickly
isolate what it means for them to do so:

\displaystyle\mathsf{LCCLiftsCompose}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{HProp}

\displaystyle\quad\to\mathsf{isLocallyCoCart}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle We extend the preceding two definitions to general families
A:X\to\mathcal{U}
by stating
that they hold for
A
if they hold for
A\circ f:\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}
for all arrows
f:\mathbb{I}\to X
(or squares
h:\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}\to X
, respectively). We overload the predicates, writing, e.g.,

\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
also for a general family
A
. A
locally cocartesian family
is one with

\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}
structure.

Lemma 3.7.

is an iso-inner fibration, then a dependent edge

Proof.

, we may pull back

. In
