Fix
b:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i
and
p:a\,0=b\,0
. Then

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{t:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i}}{\textstyle\sum_{t:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{t:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{t:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i,j:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A\,i}}t|_{\Lambda^{2}_{0}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}
is equivalent
(by innerness) to
c:\mathbb{I}\to A\,1
along with

q:c\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}=a\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,
\theta:c\circ_{q}a=b
, and
\theta_{0}:\mathsf{ap}_{-\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\theta$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\theta$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\theta$}\mathclose{}{)}}=p
. However, this is precisely the fiber of
a^{*}
, so an edge is locally cocartesian if and only
if each fiber of
a^{*}
is contractible.
∎

Theorem 3.8.

If
A:X\to\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
is iso-inner and locally cocartesian where locally cocartesian edges
compose, then locally cocartesian edges are cocartesian and
A
is cocartesian.

Proof.

We wish to show that
\Lambda^{2}_{0}\to\Delta^{2}
is orthogonal to
\tilde{A}\to X
if the

0\to 1
edge of
\Lambda^{2}_{0}
is sent to a locally cocartesian edge. Since this property can be
tested after pulling back
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}A\,x\to X
, we may assume that
X=\Delta^{2}
and concern
ourselves only with the tautological 2-simplex. Moreover, in this situation
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}A\,x
is
a (simplicial) category.

Let us now fix
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}:\Lambda^{2}_{0}\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}A\,x
which lifts
\mathsf{id}
such that
f

is locally cocartesian. Let us write
x=f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,
y=f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, and
z=g\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. With this
notation,
p:x=g\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

We wish to show that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,g,p$}\mathclose{}{]}}
extends uniquely to a 2-simplex (such a 2-simplex will
necessarily lie correctly over the unique non-degenerate 2-simplex in
X
and—since
X
is a
set—there is no interesting data in how it lies over this simplex). To this end, it suffices to
show that the following map is an equivalence
(
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
, Proposition 5.1.10)
:

f^{*}:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}

If so, we may choose the (unique) preimage of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle g,p,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle g,p,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle g,p,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle g,p,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to conclude the proof. Now, let
us choose a locally cocartesian lift of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathbb{I}\to\Delta^{2}
with starting point
y
.
This is a morphism
h^{\prime}
. Let us write
w
for the target of
h^{\prime}
. Since
h^{\prime}\circ f
is locally
cocartesian by assumption, we conclude that the following maps are equivalences by

Lemma

3.7
:

(h^{\prime}\circ f)^{*}:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}\qquad h^{\prime*}:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}

By 3-for-2, the same is then true of
f^{*}:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}
as required.
∎

In this case, locally cocartesian lifts are unique, since cocartesian lifts are.
This will be important shortly.

Lemma 3.9.

If
A:X\to\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
is iso-inner, then
\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and

\mathsf{LCCLiftsCompose}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
are propositions.

Proof.

Note that it suffices to prove that for all
x:\mathbb{I}\to X
(respectively,
x:\mathbb{I}^{2}\to X
)
that
\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
(respectively,
\mathsf{LCCLiftsCompose}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
) is
a proposition.

Only the first of these obligations is non-trivial, since
\mathsf{isLocallyCoCart}
is manifestly
valued propositions. Note that if
a
and
a^{\prime}
are both locally cocartesian lifts, then by
construction there is a unique vertical isomorphism
\iota:a\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\cong a^{\prime}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
such that

\iota\circ a=a^{\prime}
. We may then consider these as isomorphic arrows in the fiber

\mathbb{I}\times_{X}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}A\,x
, which is a category by iso-innerness. Consequently,
a=a^{\prime}

in the fiber
\mathbb{I}\times_{X}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}A\,x
whereby they are equal in
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}A\,x
.
The type of locally cocartesian lifts is therefore a proposition as required.
∎

3.2.
The directed gluing of cocartesian families

We close this section by generalizing the
directed gluing type
inspired by

Weaver and Licata 2020
and used in this context by
Gratzer et al. 2024
. Roughly, this type takes
two cocartesian families over
X
and a cocartesian functor between them and bundles them into a single
cocartesian family over
X\times\mathbb{I}
. This is a key ingredient of our proof of directed
univalence, which will eventually amount to a proof that
\mathsf{Gl}
lifts to an equivalence.

Fix cocartesians fibrations
F_{0},F_{1}\mathrel{:}X\to\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
and a cocartesian functor

\alpha\mathrel{:}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}F_{0}\,x\to F_{1}\,x
. The
directed gluing
of this data is

\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}:X\times\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}

\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}i=0\to\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We note that the fibers over
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
are given by
F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and
F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,
respectively. Moreover, for each
w:F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
there is a map over
\lambda i.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}

connecting
w
to
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,w$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. We show that
\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is iso-inner over

\mathbb{I}\times X
and that the aforementioned collection of edges make this family cocartesian
with transport functor
\alpha
.

In what follows, we assume that
X,F_{0},F_{1}
and
\alpha
are all
\flat
-annotated.
These proofs are all routine applications of orthogonality properties, combined with

Lemma

2.10
.

Lemma 3.10.

If
X
is simplicial, then
\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is iso-inner.

Proof.

First, we note by elementary manipulation of closure properties, it suffices to consider the case
where
F_{1}=\lambda\_.\,\mathbf{1}

(use the 3-for-2 fact available for iso-inner families with the factorization

\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:X}}F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}\times\mathbb{I}\to X\times\mathbb{I}
).

We must show that
\mathsf{Spine}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
is
\flat
-orthogonal to
\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}
by

Corollary

2.18
. To this end, fix a map
b\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\Delta^{n}\to X\times\mathbb{I}

along with a partial section:

s_{0}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We must show that
s_{0}
extends uniquely. Let us begin by investigating
i=\pi_{1}\circ b
, a

\flat
-annotated map
\Delta^{n}\to\mathbb{I}
. By duality, such a map corresponds to a
\flat
element of

\mathbb{I}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x_{0}\leq\dots\leq x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x_{0}\leq\dots\leq x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x_{0}\leq\dots\leq x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x_{0}\leq\dots\leq x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
so it is either
0
,
1
, or
x_{k}
for some particular
0\leq k\leq n
. If we are in the case of
i=\lambda\_.\,0
or
i=\lambda\_.\,1
, then the conclusion
is immediate from the innerness of
F_{0}
. If we are instead in the case where
i=\mathsf{dual}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x_{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x_{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x_{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x_{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we must proceed differently. We note that in such a case,
i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0
if
and only if
v_{k}=0
. This means that
s_{0}
has the type
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:\mathsf{Spine}^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and we must construct a unique extension
s
of type
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:\Delta^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:\Delta^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:\Delta^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:\Delta^{k}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v,0,\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. This is immediate by the innerness of

F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,0\dots$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
which in turn is a consequence of the innerness of
F
.

To show the “iso” part of iso-innerness, we note that this can be checked fiberwise. However,
over each fiber, this is immediate by the fact that Rezk types are an exponential ideal and
F_{0}

is iso-inner.
∎

Lemma 3.11.

If
X
is a category, then
\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is cocartesian.

Proof.

In light of
Lemma

3.10
, everything involved is a (simplicial) category.
Accordingly, we may use the LARI condition of
(
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
)
to prove this result.
Applying the results of
(
Gratzer et al. 2025
)
, we are then further reduced to constructing this
adjoint on objects.

Fix
i\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to\mathbb{I}
along with
x\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to X
,

f^{1}_{0}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and

f^{0}_{0}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0\to\alpha^{-1}_{x}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. We begin by constructing a lift of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f^{1}_{0},f^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f^{1}_{0},f^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f^{1}_{0},f^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f^{1}_{0},f^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and then we argue that it is suitably initial.

First, let us note that since
\alpha
preserves cocartesian edges, if we have
g:F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

which lies over
f:F_{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, then
x_{!}g_{0}
lies over
x_{!}f_{0}
by a contractible choice of
path since
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x_{!}g_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x_{!}g_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x_{!}g_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x_{!}g_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a cocartesian lift of the same data as
x_{!}f_{0}
. Consequently,
we may construct the desired lifts:

f^{1}=\lambda j:\mathbb{I}.\,x\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{!}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f^{1}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\quad f^{0}=\lambda j:\mathbb{I},z:i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0.\,x\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\land j$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{!}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f^{0}_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\_$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle We wish to show that there is a unique extension
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h^{1}_{0},h^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h^{1}_{0},h^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h^{1}_{0},h^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{1}_{0},h^{0}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to all of
\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}

which matches with
h_{0}
,
e
, and
f
over
p
and
q
. First, we note that we may uniquely
extend
h^{1}_{0}
to
h^{1}
since
F_{1}
is cocartesian. Let us therefore replace
h^{1}_{0}
,
f^{1}
, and

g^{1}
with
h^{1}
so that our new goal is to construct an extension of
h^{0}_{0}
given the following:

\displaystyle h^{0}_{0}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0\to\alpha^{-1}_{\chi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle f^{0}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle \displaystyle g^{0}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

To prove this, we must perform a somewhat lengthy case analysis on
\alpha
. Since it is

\flat
-annotated, we know that it is a
\flat
-element of
\mathbb{I}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x_{0},x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x_{0},x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x_{0},x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x_{0},x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
and we can analyze it
somewhat extensively.

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda\_.1
.

In this case,
\alpha=\lambda\_.1
by monotonicity, and so any extension is necessarily
trivial.

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda\_.0
.

Here, we have several sub-cases to consider:

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda\_.0
.

In this case, the condition
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0
holds in all cases, so this reduces
precisely to the fact that
F_{0}
is cocartesian. In particular, we note that we may extend

h^{0}
in
F_{0}
(not in the fiber) using the fact that
f^{0}
is cocartesian. This extension
is unique by construction and, since the input to the extension lies over
h^{1}
, it lives
in the correct fiber (uniquely).

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda j.\,j
.

In this case, we must construct a lift of the following type:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to k\land j=0\to\alpha^{-1}_{\chi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h^{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h^{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h^{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Given
k,j:\mathbb{I}
, since everything is simplicial we may assume that
k\leq j
or
j\leq k
. In other words, our condition is equivalent to
k=0
or
j=0
; any extension is
fully determined by the boundary conditions.

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda\_.1
.

In this case,
\alpha=\lambda\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,k
and so we may just take
h_{0}=h^{0}_{0}
.

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda j\,.j
.

Here, we have several sub-cases to consider:

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda j.\,j
.

In this case,
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}=j
and so we may simply take
h^{0}=f^{0}
.

Case.:

\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\lambda\_.1
.

In this case,
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}=k\lor j
, so our condition
\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j$}\mathclose{}{)}}=0
amounts
to
k=0\land j=0
. Consequently, we may take
h=f^{0}_{0}
. ∎

Corollary 3.12.

Cocartesian transport from

\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to
\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}

is given by
\alpha
.

Proof.

First, we note that given
f:\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}\cong F_{0}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, there is a
functorial choice of edges:

\lambda i.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}},\lambda\_.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}},\lambda\_.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}},\lambda\_.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}},\lambda\_.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}

To show the desired identification, it suffices to show that this edge is cocartesian and, using
the standard result that a natural transformation is an equivalence if and only if it is pointwise
such, we restrict our attention to the case where
c\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C
. This, however, is
immediate in light of the above proof—in particular, cocartesian transport along a constant edge
in
C
is trivial.
∎

Corollary 3.13.

The projection map
\pi_{0}:\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F_{0},F_{1},\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to F_{1}\circ\pi_{0}
is a cocartesian
functor over
X\times\mathbb{I}
.

4.
The universe of amazingly cocartesian types

We now turn to the construction of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
. As mentioned in
the introduction,
{\mathsf{Cat}}
will be a subtype of
\mathcal{U}
and therefore must be classified by a proposition

\mathcal{U}\to\mathsf{HProp}
. The most obvious choice of proposition is something akin to being
cocartesian,
but a moment’s thought reveals this is unworkable: if we are to define a map
\mathsf{isCocartFib}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathsf{HProp}
, what should the input be cocartesian over? Cocartesianness is a property of
families!

To fix this, we follow
Licata et al. 2018
as refined in the context of directed type
theories
(
Weaver and Licata 2020
;
Gratzer et al. 2024
)
. First, consider a general notion of fibration

\mathsf{isFib}_{X}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}^{X}\to\mathsf{HProp}
. The goal is to define a predicate that
witnesses fibrancy of a type
A
viewed as a family over the entire ambient context. From

\mathsf{isFib}_{\mathbb{I}}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathsf{HProp}
,
Lemma

2.6
yields
precisely such a notion of fibrancy. Moreover, this stronger notion of fibration can be shown to
agree with the classical notion when we restrict attention to
\flat
-annotated families.
We will now apply this construction, leveraging
Theorem

3.8
.

Let us write
i:\Delta^{2}\to\mathbb{I}^{2}
for the canonical inclusion.
Using
Lemmas

2.6
and
3.9
, we now transpose
\mathsf{isInner}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,

\mathsf{hasLCCLifts}
, and
\mathsf{LCCLiftsCompose}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to obtain elements of
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}
, namely
\mathsf{aisInner}
,
\mathsf{aHasLCCLifts}
, and
\mathsf{aLCCLiftsCompose}
. Here we have
used
i
as, e.g.,
\mathsf{isInner}
takes
\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{U}
not
\mathbb{I}^{2}\to\mathcal{U}
.
We then define
{\mathsf{Cat}}
:

{\mathsf{Cat}}\coloneq\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{isRezk}\,A\times\mathsf{aIsInner}\,A\\
{}\times\mathsf{aHasLCCLifts}\,A\times\mathsf{aLCCLiftsCompose}\,A\end{array}

Lemma 4.1.

{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a subtype of
\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
.

\univcocart

Proof.

Fix
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
. Our goal is to show that
A
factors through
{\mathsf{Cat}}
if
and only if
A
is cocartesian. To prove this, let us consider the data involved in a
factorization through
{\mathsf{Cat}}
. By definition, this is equivalent to factoring through four distinct
subobjects of
\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}
: those carved out by
\mathsf{isRezk}
,
\mathsf{aIsInner}
,

\mathsf{aHasLCCLifts}
, and
\mathsf{aLCCLiftsCompose}
. In the latter three cases, we may analyze
these subobjects using the transpositions used to define them.

For instance, if
A
factors through
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{B:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}{\textstyle\sum_{B:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{B:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{B:\mathcal{U}_{\boxbslash}}}\mathsf{aIsInner}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, then there is
an element of the following type by
Lemma

2.6
:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}\mathsf{isInner}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}\mathsf{isInner}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}\mathsf{isInner}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}}}\mathsf{isInner}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\circ x\circ i$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}

Such an element exists if and only if
A
is an inner fibration.

This reasoning applies to
\mathsf{aHasLCCLifts}
and
\mathsf{aLCCLiftsCompose}
so we may conclude
that
A
factors through
{\mathsf{Cat}}
if and only if it is iso-inner, locally cocartesian, and locally
cocartesian edges compose. The desired bi-implication is then

Theorem

3.8
.
∎

5.
The category of categories

In this section, we leverage
Section

1.1
to prove the crucial properties of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
.
Namely, we prove
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is Segal and Rezk, satisfies directed univalence as described in

Section

1
, and is simplicial. Combining these results together we show that
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a
category and, in particular, the category of categories.

5.1.
Classifying cocartesian fibrations

The main input to the proofs that
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is Segal and Rezk
is a characterization of cocartesian fibrations over

\Delta^{n}\times C
where
C
is a category. To see why, note that by

Section

1.1
, we know that
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\times\Delta^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is
determined by a cocartesian family over
X\times\Delta^{n}
. By giving a precise description of
such families, we obtain a more tractable version of, e.g., the restriction map

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
.
This version will be manifestly invertible, and so we can conclude that
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is Segal.
A key lemma in this process is the following:

Lemma 5.1.

For
X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
a category and
A,B\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\to\mathcal{U}
cocartesian,
a cocartesian functor
\alpha:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\textstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x:X}}A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to B\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
induces an equivalence of
total categories
\widetilde{\alpha}:\widetilde{A}\simeq\widetilde{B}
iff

\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{x\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X}}{\textstyle\prod_{x\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{x\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{x\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X}}\mathsf{isEquiv}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
holds.

Proof.

Since cocartesian families are isofibrations, we know that
\widetilde{A}
and

\widetilde{B}
are both categories themselves. By
Lemma

2.9
, we
check that
\widetilde{\alpha}
is fully faithful, and essentially surjective.

Essential surjectivity is straightforward: given
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\widetilde{B}
, we
take
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,\alpha\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
as
\alpha
is invertible on
\flat

elements of
X
. To show that
\widetilde{\alpha}
is fully faithful, note that
transport induces an equivalence between
\mathbb{I}\to\widetilde{A}
and
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:\mathbb{I}\to X}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:\mathbb{I}\to X}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{a_{0}:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}},a_{1}:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{a_{0}:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\,0$}\mathclose{}{)}},a_{1}:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle and

is invertible.
∎

. To

Lemma 5.2.

Given

as above, the transport map

\alpha=\lambda x.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{!}

is a cocartesian functor

.

Proof.

Unfolding, this follows from the 3-for-2 condition which holds for cocartesian

(
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
, Proposition 5.1.8)
.
∎

:

a:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to
A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and, if

to
begin with, record the original
a
as well.

Lemma 5.3.

\mathsf{glue}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{p:X\times\mathbb{I}}}{\textstyle\prod_{p:X\times\mathbb{I}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{p:X\times\mathbb{I}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{p:X\times\mathbb{I}}}A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to B\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a cocartesian functor.

Proof.

Following
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
, to check that
\mathsf{glue}
is cocartesian, it
suffices to check that the Beck-Chevalley natural transformation is invertible. By

Lemma

2.8
, it suffices to check this on
\flat
elements where it is
immediate.
∎

Corollary 5.4.

\mathsf{glue}
is an equivalence of cocartesian families.

Proof.

Applying
Lemma

5.1
, it suffices to check this equivalence fiberwise on

\flat
-annotated elements
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,i$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\times\mathbb{I}
. In particular, it suffices to
check that induces an equivalence on
\flat
-annotated elements of
\mathbb{I}
which, by

Section

2.3
, consists only of
0
and
1
. However, over
0
and
1
we see that

\mathsf{glue}
is an equivalence: over
0
, this is immediate and over
1
it follows from the
observation that cocartesian transport over the identity arrow is the identity.
∎

Thus, a cocartesian family
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\times\mathbb{I}\to\mathcal{U}
is fully described by its
restrictions to
0
and
1
along with the associated transport map. Combining this classification
result in the case where
X=\mathbf{1}
with
Section

1.1
, we obtain the
directed univalence principle:

\dua

We note that, in general, we actually obtain an equivalence between

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid X\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid X\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid X\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid X\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}
.
For what follows, we also require a similar accounting of cocartesian families

A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}X\times\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{U}
. The story plays out in much the same way; we define
B_{1}

as the following iterated glue type:

\displaystyle B_{0}=\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle
