We may then check directly that this assignment preserves cocartesian arrows by unfolding their
construction in the
\mathsf{Gl}
and checking this holds on global data once more. In the end, it
amounts to the proof that
\mathsf{glue}
preserves cocartesian edges; when restricted to the global edges

0\leq 1
,
1\leq 2
and
2\leq 3
we find that the above characterization of
B
collapses
to a single application of
\mathsf{Gl}
along various cocartesian functors.
∎

Again, an application of
Lemma

5.1
allows us to conclude that
\mathsf{glue}_{2}
is an
equivalence. Combining these steps once more with
Section

1.1
once more, we conclude
the following:

Corollary 5.6.

Cocartesian transport induces an equivalence

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\simeq\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}\times A_{1}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{2}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}\times A_{1}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{2}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}\times A_{1}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{2}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{X}}}A_{0}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{1}\times A_{1}\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A_{2}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}
.

5.2.
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is Segal and Rezk

Having characterized both cocartesian fibrations over
X\times\Delta^{2}
and
X\times\mathbb{I}
for
all categories
X
, it is only slightly more work to prove that
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is both Segal and Rezk.

Lemma 5.7.

{\mathsf{Cat}}
is Segal.

Proof.

We wish to show that
{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\Delta^{2}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\Lambda^{2}_{1}}
is an equivalence. Using

Section

2.3
, it suffices to show that the following map is an equivalence for all

n
:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
.

Note that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
is equivalent to the
following:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\times_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

This follows from the fact that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
has an internal right adjoint (
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sharp\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
)
together with the fact that
\Lambda^{2}_{1}=\mathbb{I}\mathrel{\textstyle\sqcup_{\mathbf{1}}}\mathbb{I}
. Next, since
\mathbb{I}^{n}

is a category, the results of the previous section allow us to rephrase the above map into the
following:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B\times B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B\times B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B\times B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B,C:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B\times B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}\to

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}\times_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\ This being an equivalence follows immediately from the fact that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
preserves pullbacks by
virtue of
Section

2.2
.
∎

Inspecting this proof, we see that if
P:\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
, the resulting composed edge

f:\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a cocartesian fibration where
f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}=P\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,

f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}=P\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and cocartesian transport from
0
to
1
is the composite of
transporting in
P
from
\bar{0}
to
\bar{1}
and then from
\bar{1}
to
\bar{2}
.

Lemma 5.8.

{\mathsf{Cat}}
is Rezk.

Proof.

We wish to show that all synthetic isomophisms in
{\mathsf{Cat}}
are equivalent to the identity. Once
more, we apply
Section

2.3
showing that the restriction map

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{E}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{E}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{E}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{E}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
is an
equivalence. Commuting
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
with the pushout defining
\mathbb{E}
once more, we may instead consider
the map:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\times_{\dots}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\mathbb{I}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\times_{\dots}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}\times\Delta^{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

Applying the results of the previous section and commuting
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
past pullbacks, we
may recast the above:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}f\circ g=\mathsf{id}\times h\circ f=\mathsf{id}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}f\circ g=\mathsf{id}\times h\circ f=\mathsf{id}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}f\circ g=\mathsf{id}\times h\circ f=\mathsf{id}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}^{n}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:A\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\textstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{g,h:B\to^{\mathrm{cc}}A}}f\circ g=\mathsf{id}\times h\circ f=\mathsf{id}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}

However, since
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a subtype of
\mathcal{U}
it satisfies the univalence axiom.
The result then follows from the observation that the data imposed on
f
ensures
that it is a family of equivalences.
∎

5.3.
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is simplicial

Our remaining task is to show that
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is simplicial. Unfortunately, this is far from immediately
obvious. After all,
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is defined using the amazing right adjoint to
\mathbb{I}\to-
, which is the
primary source of non-simplicial types. Fortunately, in this case we may use

Section

1.1
to produce a left section to
\eta:{\mathsf{Cat}}\to\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
and this
implies that
\eta
is an equivalence
(
Rijke et al. 2020
, Lemma 1.20)
.

To begin with, we record the following lemma:

Lemma 5.9.

The commuting square between
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}

and
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}\to\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
induced by
\eta
is cartesian.

Proof.

Unfolding definitions and comparing fibers, this follows from the fact that each

A:{\mathsf{Cat}}
is simplicial and
\boxbslash
is lex.
∎

Next, we note that if
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}\to\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
represents a cocartesian family itself,
then there must be a unique classifying map
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
and, pasting together the relevant
pullback squares, we obtain the following composite pullback diagram:

{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}
{\mathsf{Cat}}
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}
{\mathsf{Cat}}

By the univalence property of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
, this bottom composite must be the identity. Consequently, the
classifying map
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is the required left inverse to the unit. All that remains,
therefore, is to prove that
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}\to\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a cocartesian fibration.

To prove this we will use
Section

2.3
along with

Lemmas

2.10
and
3.4
.
First, we must note that
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
and
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}
are both
categories by
Corollary

2.21
.

Lemma 5.10.

The family
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}\to\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
is cocartesian.

Proof.

As a map between categories, it is automatically iso-inner and simplicial. It therefore suffices
to prove that the comparison map
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathbb{I}}\times_{\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}}\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}
has a left adjoint right inverse. For concision, we write

C=\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
and, commute
\boxbslash
with
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum}{\textstyle\sum}{\scriptstyle\sum}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum}
to replace
\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}
with

E=\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\hat{\boxbslash}A
in what follows.

We may use
Lemma

2.10
so that it suffices to check that
this property holds when restricted to
\flat
-annotated elements of
C^{\mathbb{I}}\times_{C}E
.
Accordingly, we may fix
A\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to C
along with an element

a_{0}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}A\,0
. By
Section

2.3
, we may assume that
A=\eta\circ A^{\prime}

and
a_{0}=\eta\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a_{0}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{0}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a_{0}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a_{0}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
for some unique
A^{\prime}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
and

a_{0}^{\prime}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}A

Since
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\bullet}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is cocartesian, we lift
A^{\prime},a_{0}^{\prime}
to a cocartesian
morphism
a^{\prime}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A^{\prime}\,i
with
p^{\prime}:a^{\prime}\,0=a_{0}^{\prime}
. We now
argue that
a=\eta\circ a^{\prime}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i:\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hat{\boxbslash}A\,i

and the induced equality
p:a\,0=a_{0}
is the desired universal lift over
A
,
a
.

After appropriately massaging the input data, it suffices to show that if we are given

H\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\Delta^{2}\to\boxbslash{\mathsf{Cat}}
and a lift of this map
h\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle t:\Lambda^{2}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle t:\Lambda^{2}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle t:\Lambda^{2}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t:\Lambda^{2}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hat{\boxbslash}H\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. There are a handful of paths relating these two
a
and
A
,
but after induction we may definitionally identify (1)
H\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}
with
A
(2)
h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}
with

a
. We must show that
h
extends uniquely to some
\bar{h}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle t:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle t:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle t:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t:\Delta^{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\hat{\boxbslash}H\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. By
Section

2.3
, we may once more factor
H
and
h
through

{\mathsf{Cat}}
whereby the result is an immediate consequence of our construction of
a^{\prime}
as a
cocartesian lift.
∎

Corollary 5.11.

{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a category.

6.
Full straightening-unstraightening

In this section, we prove the Lurie’s straightening–unstraightening theorem which states that for a
category
C\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
, the type
C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is equivalent to the subcategory of

{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
(that is,
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathbb{I}}}}f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}=C
) restricted such that its 0- and
1-cells are given cocartesian families and cocartesian functors. Accordingly, for the remainder of
this section let us fix
C\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
a category.

To do this, we will construct a map
U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
and prove that it is (1)
an embedding such that (2) its image on
\flat
-annotated elements
C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
, and
\mathbb{I}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
satisfies precisely the above criteria. From this, we show that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
satisfies the expected universal property for the subcategory
\mathsf{Cocart}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of

cocartesian families over
C
(
Corollary

6.4
).

{remark}

The material in this section closely follows
Cisinski et al. 2025
with only minor alterations to
make it more convenient in
TT
\boxbslash
. In particular, it is from there that we learned of this
method of constructing the unstraightening functor and characterizing its image. That such an
adaptation is possible is expected but encouraging: the axiomatic approach given by op. cit. is
intended to give high-level arguments which can be translated into formal systems satisfying their
axioms and our construction of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
ensures that
TT
\boxbslash
satisfies all the relevant axioms for
this argument.

{remark}

We avoid explicitly constructing
\mathsf{Cocart}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}
merely to avoid the
detour of describing the construction of non-full subcategories. Such constructions are possible
using e.g.,
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\mathbb{I}}
.

6.1.
The unstraightening map

We begin by constructing a map
U
from
C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
to
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
. We break this process
into several steps. We begin by considering two particular cocartesian families over
C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
:

E\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}\qquad B\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}=C

These are both cocartesian families over
C
and the canonical projection is a cocartesian map

\pi_{0}:E\to^{\mathrm{cc}}B
as well—and therefore a cocartesian functor. We may
therefore glue these together to form a cocartesian family:

\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
. First, let us compute

\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and observe that it is canonically identified to
\lambda\_.\,C
, via the following family of paths:

\Phi=\lambda f.\,\mathsf{ua}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{f:C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{f:C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{f:C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{f:C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,1$}\mathclose{}{)}}=C

By transposing and using this identification, we obtain:

\displaystyle U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}

\displaystyle U=\lambda f.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}},\Phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}},\Phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}},\Phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Gl}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,B,\pi_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,-$}\mathclose{}{)}},\Phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

6.2.
The image of the unstraightening map

Our next task to identify the image of
U
and, in particular, to show that it is precisely the
category of cocartesian families over
C
and cocartesian functors between them. We therefore
compute fibers of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
.

In particular, we will show that the fiber over
p\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
is precisely the
proposition stating whether
p
is cocartesian and over
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}

it corresponds to the triple of propositions requiring
f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and
f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to be cocartesian
and
f
itself to induce a map of cocartesian families. In light of the Segal condition, this
characters the fibers over arbitrary simplices and, via
Section

2.3
and the
simpliciality of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
, proves that
U
is an embedding. Moreover, our description of the fibers
shows that the resultant subcategory of
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
is precisely as described at the beginning
of this section.

Lemma 6.1.

The fiber of
U
over
p\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
is a proposition inhabited iff
p
is cocartesian.

Proof.

Post-composing with directed univalence, we may identify a fiber of
U
with a fiber of
the map:

U^{\prime}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}

Consider a category
E\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}
and a function
\pi_{E}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}E\to C
. Our goal
is to compute the fiber
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}}U^{\prime}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathsf{mod}_{\flat}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Unfolding
U^{\prime}
, an element
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is sent to
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,\pi_{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
if and only if we have
an equivalence
e\mathrel{:_{\flat}}E\simeq\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and an equation

p\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\pi_{E}=\pi_{1}\circ e
. By another application of univalence, this is
equivalent to requiring that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
, so the fiber amounts to

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\pi_{E}^{-1}=f$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
. This is a proposition by

Proposition

2.5
and by
Section

1.1
inhabited iff
\pi_{E}
is a
cocartesian family.
∎

Lemma 6.2.

The fiber of
U
over
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
is a proposition inhabited iff

f
is is a cocartesian functor between cocartesian families.

Proof.

Rearranging equations, we may identify
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
with

\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{h:\Delta^{2}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}}}h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\lang which we may then identify via
directed univalence with

\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}
. Post-composing
U
with these
maps, we instead consider the following:

U^{\prime}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{E,F:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}E\to F\times F\to C$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}

Consider categories
E,F\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}
and functions
\pi_{F}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}F\to C
and

\alpha\mathrel{:_{\flat}}E\to F
. Our goal is to compute the fiber of
U^{\prime}
over this data.

Unfolding, an element
h\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}
is sent to
E,F,\pi_{F}
if and only if we
have the following:

•

an equivalence
e_{0}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}E\simeq\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,

•

an equivalence
e_{1}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}F\simeq\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\textstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{c:C}}h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,

•

a path
\phi\mathrel{:_{\flat}}e_{1}\circ\alpha=\beta\circ e_{0}
where

\beta=\lambda c.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{!}
is given by the cocartesian transport of
h

•

a path
\phi\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\pi_{F}=\pi_{0}\circ e_{1}
.

Once more using univalence, we are reduced to
\flat
-annotated equation between maps of families
over
C
(i.e.
C\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}B^{A}
):

\displaystyle\lambda c.\,\pi_{0}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:\pi_{F}^{-1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle
