\displaystyle\lambda c.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{!}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\textstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{c:C}}h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to h\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Since
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
embeds into
\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid C\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}B^{A}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid C\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}B^{A}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid C\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}B^{A}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\big\langle}{\langle}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid C\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A,B:\mathcal{U}}}B^{A}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\big\rangle}{\rangle}}}
by
directed univalence,
Proposition

2.5
ensures this fiber over
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle E,F,\pi_{F}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle E,F,\pi_{F}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle E,F,\pi_{F}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E,F,\pi_{F}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a
proposition. The same analysis shows that it is inhabited iff
\pi_{F}\circ\alpha
and

\pi_{E}
are cocartesian and
\alpha
is a map of cocartesian families.
∎

Corollary 6.3.

The map
U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
is an embedding.

Proof.

To show that
U
is an embedding, we will show that

\Delta_{U}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

is an equivalence. Applying
Section

2.3
along with the fact that all of the objects
involved here are simplicial, it suffices to show that the following map is an equivalence:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\Delta^{n}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

Since both sides of this are categories, we may restrict to the case where
n=0,1
. In this
case, it suffices to show that if

f,g\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\Delta^{n}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and
p\mathrel{:_{\flat}}U\circ f=U\circ g

then there is a path
(f,{\mathsf{refl}})=(g,p)
. However, this is precisely equivalent to asking that
the fiber of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U\circ-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U\circ-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U\circ-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U\circ-$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\dagger}
over
\mathsf{mod}_{\flat}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is contractible. By previous
results, we know the fiber is a proposition and it is inhabited by
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f,{\mathsf{refl}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Consequently,
it is contractible as required.
∎

In fact, in light of our identification of the fibers of
U
we may also characterize when a functor
lifts along it. This is, in essence, the universal property of
\mathsf{Cocart}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}
mentioned earlier:

Corollary 6.4 (Straightening–unstraightening).

If
D\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathcal{U}
is a category, a map
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}D\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
lifts
along
U
to
{\mathsf{Cat}}^{C}
if and only if

(1)

for each
d\mathrel{:_{\flat}}D
, the functor
f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a cocartesian family.

(2)

for each
d\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to D
, the functor induced by
f\circ d:\mathbb{I}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}
is a cocartesian functor between the cocartesian families.

Proof.

Our goal is to characterize for which
f
the following map is an equivalence:

D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to D

Notably, we know already this map is an embedding (it is the pullback of
U
) and so we merely
wish to characterize when it is surjective. Using
Section

2.3
along with the fact
that both sides are categories, it suffices to consider when the following maps are surjective:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D\times_{{\mathsf{Cat}}_{/C}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C\to{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to D$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}

We now unfold these maps and use
Proposition

2.5
. These guarantee that the first map
will hit
d\mathrel{:_{\flat}}D
if and only if
f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a cocartesian family. Similarly,
the second map will hit
d\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathbb{I}\to D
if and only if
f\circ d
is a cocartesian
functor between cocartesian families.
∎

7.
Examples

We have thus far focused on the construction of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
and verifying its essential
properties and so we close by discussing some of the new examples and category theory unlocked by
{\mathsf{Cat}}
.
We content ourselves with only sketching several examples.

7.1.
Subcategories of
{\mathsf{Cat}}

We begin by noting that since every covariant family is cocartesian, there is a unique map from the
base of the universal covariant family
{\mathcal{S}}
to the base of the universal cocartesian family

{\mathsf{Cat}}
. This is the inclusion of groupoids into categories.

Lemma 7.1.

The map
i:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is fully faithful and possesses both left and right adjoints:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\dashv i\dashv\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}
.

Proof Sketch.

The second half of this statements follows from

Lemma

2.10
. In particular, we use this lemma to extend the
pointwise assignments of
X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}\mapsto\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid X$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}:{\mathcal{S}}
and

X\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}\mapsto\mathop{\bigcirc_{\mathsf{grpd}}}X:{\mathcal{S}}
to functors
{\mathsf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{S}}
. The fact that
i

is fully faithful is then immediate from
Section

2.3
: if
X
is a groupoid then
the unit
X\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid i\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
is an equivalence. It is a standard argument that
a unit being invertible implies the left adjoint is fully faithful.
∎

Many other interesting categories exist as full subcategories of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
. For instance, we may
isolate univalent 1-categories as the full subcategory of
{\mathsf{Cat}}

(
Gratzer et al. 2024
, §7)
given by the
following predicate:

\displaystyle\mathsf{is1Cat}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid{\mathsf{Cat}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{HProp}

\displaystyle\mathsf{is1Cat}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{a,b\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C}}{\textstyle\prod_{a,b\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{a,b\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{a,b\mathrel{:_{\flat}}C}}\mathsf{isSet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\hom_{C}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\hom_{C}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hom_{C}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hom_{C}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Similar definitions immediately yield
(n,1)
-categories for all
n
. Notably, by restricting to
n=-1
we obtain the category of partial orders and, restricting further to linear partial orders,
the simplex category
\Delta\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{Cat}}
. In fact, the same argument as was used to
{\mathcal{S}}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{Cat}}
allows us to prove the following:

Lemma 7.2.

The inclusion of
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{n}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{Cat}}
is a right adjoint.

Proof Sketch.

One adapts
Lemma

7.1
to use the modality nullifying the maps
\Lambda^{2}_{1}\to\Delta^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\to\mathbf{1}
, and
\partial\Delta^{n+2}\to\Delta^{n+2}
.
∎

Towards algebraic K-theory

For a small example of how these ingredients might be combined to build a useful and important
construction in higher category theory, we turn our attention to monoidal categories.
Let us write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}
for the element of
\Delta
realizing the linear order

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0\leq\dots\leq n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 0\leq\dots\leq n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq\dots\leq n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq\dots\leq n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
. Using
Section

1.1
, we define

\rho_{n}^{i}:\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
which sends
0\leq 1
to

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle i\leq i+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle i\leq i+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\leq i+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\leq i+1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\subseteq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}
.

Definition 7.3.

A monoidal category
C^{\otimes}:{\mathsf{Cat}}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}
is a functor
where

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{n},\dots,\rho^{n}_{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\rho^{1}_{n},\dots,\rho^{n}_{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rho^{1}_{n},\dots,\rho^{n}_{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rho^{1}_{n},\dots,\rho^{n}_{n}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:C^{\otimes}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to C^{\otimes}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\dots\times C^{\otimes}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

is an equivalence for all
n
.

Replacing
{\mathsf{Cat}}
by
{\mathcal{S}}
in the above gives the definition of an
E_{1}
-monoid: a
homotopy-coherent monoid
(
Gratzer et al. 2024
, §7)
.

Definition 7.4.

The category of monoidal categories
\mathsf{MCat}
is the full subcategory of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
spanned by monoidal categories.

We readily adapt this definition to (1) the category of
E_{1}
-monoids
\mathsf{Mon}
as a subcategory
of
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}
and to (2) the category of monoidal 1-categories
\mathsf{MCat}_{1}
as
a subcategory of
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{1}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}
.

As both
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}:{\mathsf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{S}}
and the inclusion
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{1}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
are right adjoints,
they preserve finite products and therefore post-composing by these maps induces functors

\mathsf{MCat}\to\mathsf{Mon}
and
\mathsf{MCat}_{1}\to\mathsf{MCat}
. We note next that—viewing
\mathsf{Mon}

as a subcategory of
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\Delta$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}}
—we may take the colimit of

M:\mathsf{Mon}
to obtain a space
\mathchoice{\textstyle\varinjlim}{\textstyle\varinjlim}{\scriptstyle\varinjlim}{\scriptscriptstyle\varinjlim}M
. In fact, this space is canonically pointed: the
initial object in
\mathsf{Mon}
is the functor
\mathsf{const}\,\mathbf{1}
and

\mathchoice{\textstyle\varinjlim}{\textstyle\varinjlim}{\scriptstyle\varinjlim}{\scriptscriptstyle\varinjlim}\mathsf{const}\,\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{1}
. Finally, regarding the loop-space functor as a map

\Omega:{\mathcal{S}}_{*}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{*}
we define
k
to be the following chain of functors:

k:\mathsf{MCat}_{1}\to\mathsf{MCat}\to\mathsf{Mon}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{*}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{*}

We may now define the simplest form of algebraic K-theory:

Definition 7.5 (Quillen).

The
i
th K-group
of a monoidal 1-category
C^{\otimes}
is the
i
th homotopy group

K_{i}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\pi_{i}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C^{\otimes}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Notably, with
{\mathsf{Cat}}
to hand all of these definitions are quite conceptual and automatically
functorial. We emphasize that this is only a first step towards realizing K-theory. We leave it to
future work to show e.g., that modules over a ring are a monoidal category.

7.2.
The structure homomorphism principle

To give a different class of examples involving
{\mathsf{Cat}}
, we turn to the
structure homomorphism
principle
. This is the directed enhancement of
HoTT
’s structure identity principle. This
principle states that by taking ordinary type-theoretic definitions of objects in a certain
category but using
{\mathsf{Cat}}
or
{\mathcal{S}}
instead of
\mathcal{U}
, we obtain the correct synthetic category
with the expected homomorphisms.

For a simplest example of this phenomena:

Lemma 7.6.

The type
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A
is the
lax
slice
\mathbf{1}\mathbin{/\kern-3.0pt/}{\mathsf{Cat}}
i.e. its objects
are pointed categories
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and when
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A

morphisms
\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C,c$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle D,d$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
consist of functions
f:C\to D
together with a morphism

\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c$}\mathclose{}{)}},d$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Proof.

As
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A
is the total space of the cocartesian family
{\mathsf{Cat}}\to\mathcal{U}
, it is a
category. The characterization of objects is immediate from
Proposition

2.5
. For
morphisms, we use
Section

1.1
with the factorization of a morphism in a total space of a
cocartesian family into a cocartesian map followed by a vertical map.
∎

More generally, one may show that
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A^{C}
is the lax slice category
C\mathbin{/\kern-3.0pt/}{\mathsf{Cat}}

for any
C\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}
.

For a more sophisticated example, consider the following type built using the right adjoint to
i:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
.

{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\mathrm{smarked}}=\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

By directed univalence applied to
{\mathcal{S}}
, this type is equivalent to
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\hom\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq},{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq},{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq},{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq},{\mathsf{Bool}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
which is easily seen to be a category.
Objects of
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\mathrm{smarked}}
are a pairs of (1) a category
C\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}
and (2)
a (decidable) predicate
\phi_{C}
on the groupoid
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
(recall that

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}
extends
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\flat\mid-$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
). The morphisms in
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\mathrm{smarked}}
are then
functors between the underlying category
f:C\to D
such that
\phi_{C}=\phi_{D}\circ f
. This is
almost the category of
marked
categories (the category of categories with a distinguished class of
morphisms along with functors preserving these morphisms) but the morphisms are off: we should
expect only that
\phi_{C}
implies
\phi_{D}\circ f
.

To rectify this, we should replace
{\mathsf{Bool}}
with the non-discrete category
\mathbb{I}
. Just as with

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}A
, this introduces the required laxity in the morphism. Unfortunately, however, we
can no longer rely on directed univalence for
{\mathcal{S}}
in this case; we must use
Section

1.1

which only applies to
\flat
-annotated elements. This makes it much more difficult to prove
that
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a category. Systematically handling
these “mixed-variance” applications of SHP requires more exploration of exponentiable
functors
(
Bardomiano Martínez 2025a
)
: we must show that (co)cartesian functors are
exponentiable. We defer this to future work and instead work relative to the following conjecture:

Conjecture 7.7.

If
C\mathrel{:_{\flat}}{\mathsf{Cat}}
then
-\to C:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathsf{Cat}}
is cartesian where the cartesian lift of
f\mathrel{:_{\flat}}\hom_{{\mathcal{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}

to
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,\phi\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,\phi\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,\phi\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,\phi\circ f$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

The difficult piece of this conjecture is establishing that
-\to C
is an iso-inner family, with
the cartesian lifts following directly from the dual of
Proposition

3.4
. To see this
enables further applications of SHP, note that choosing
C=\mathbb{I}
yields:

Lemma 7.8.

The category
{\mathsf{Cat}}_{\mathrm{marked}}=\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{I}\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is
the category of marked categories.

Proof.

We discuss only the characterization of synthetic morphisms. First, we note that the projection

\pi_{0}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{C:{\mathsf{Cat}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\to is a pullback of the family described in

7.7
and therefore cartesian. We may then factor any morphism

f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi_{C},\phi_{D}\circ\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\simeq}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. This is precisely the data of a morphism of marked categories.
∎

8.
Conclusions and related work

In this work, we have constructed a subtype of the universe
{\mathsf{Cat}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}
and shown
that it gives rise to the
category of categories
within
STT
. In particular, we have shown
that it is simplicial, Segal, and Rezk and characterized its objects and mapping spaces to be
categories and functors. To give an even more precise characterization of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
, we then prove
Lurie’s straightening–unstraightening theorem. Finally, we show how results from

\infty
-category theory can now be proved (e.g., various adjunctions between
{\mathcal{S}}
and

{\mathsf{Cat}}
) in short order and use the particular nature of type theory (in the form of the
SHP) to construct new
\infty
-categories quickly and intuitively.

8.1.
Related work

There has been a large amount of work on both directed approaches to type theory and
straightening–unstraightening generally. Our work fits into the broader tradition of directed type
theory, specifically the line of work initiated by
Riehl and Shulman 2017
on simplicial type theory
(
Bardomiano Martínez 2025b
;
Bardomiano Martínez 2025a
;
Buchholtz and Weinberger 2023
;
Riehl 2025
;
Shulman 2019
;
Weinberger 2024b
;
Weinberger 2024a
;
Weinberger 2022
;
Gratzer et al. 2025
)
.

In this specific context,
{\mathsf{Cat}}
fills a major missing piece in the foundations of
STT
and allows
for new applications to, for instance, higher algebra and monoidal category theory. For directed
type theory generally, we believe this construction should make it possible to more directly provide
semantics to “fully directed” type theories
(
Licata and Harper 2011
;
Warren 2013
;
Nuyts 2015
;
North 2018
;
Kavvos 2019
;
Nuyts 2020
;
Ahrens et al. 2023
;
Neumann and Altenkirch 2024
;
Neumann 2025b
;
Neumann 2025a
)
.
In particular, a model of those type theories which adequately models
\infty
-categories can now be
constructed by a syntactic translation to
TT
\boxbslash
. The presence of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
, in particular, means that
the features supported by
TT
\boxbslash
should be adequate to encode all of those presently considered.

Other approaches to amazing right adjoints

The use of the right adjoint to
\mathbb{I}\to-
to construct a universal family with suitable structure
dates back to
Licata et al. 2018
, where it was used in the context of cubical type theory. While,
for instance,
Riley 2024
proposed a more refined version of this technique which gave a more
judgmental account of the amazing right adjoint, our approach closely follows
Licata et al. 2018

and, especially, its adaptation to directed type theory by
Weaver and Licata 2020
. In particular,
op. cit. use this methodology to construct a directed univalent universe of groupoids in
bicubical type theory.
Gratzer et al. 2024
then introduced
TT
\boxbslash
in order to adapt this
methodology to apply to standard
\infty
-categories and further prove that the universe of
groupoids is a category. Our work carries this program further forward by constructing not just a
category of groupoids via a universal covariant family, but a category of categories via a universal
cocartesian family. This is a substantial step—cocartesian families enjoy fewer nice properties
than covariant families and this complicates various aspects of the proof e.g., the argument
that
{\mathsf{Cat}}
is simplicial.

Existing proofs of straightening–unstraightening

Since Lurie’s original proof
(
Lurie 2009
)
, two additional proofs have been published in the
literature. The first is given by
Hebestreit et al. 2025
and is a much more directed and succinct
version of Lurie’s proof, but still employs the same fundamental approach. In
particular, the main result is a Quillen equivalence of two model categories rather than an
intrinsically
\infty
-categorical or synthetic approach.

More closely related is the work of
Cisinski and Nguyen 2022
which develops a universal cocartesian
family as we do and uses this to prove straightening–unstraightening, directed univalence, and
similar. The proof strategy of our approach is quite similar to op. cit.: a straightforward proof of
the universal fibration and a more lengthy argument that its base is a category. In fact, op. cit.
is influenced by various standard techniques in the semantics of type theory. A crucial difference
between our approaches, however, is the ambient framework used to manage
\infty
-categories. Unlike
our approach,
Cisinski and Nguyen 2022
use quasicategories along with various model categorical tools
from the Joyal and marked model structures. This is in contrast with our more synthetic approach,
which uses model categories only indirectly via
Theorem

2.7
.

Particularly in light of the last proof, we feel that our approach is an interesting example in
how higher category theory can influence the development of type theory (through
HoTT
) which can
in turn contribute new techniques and proofs to higher category theory. We also hope that the
model-independent nature of our proof will result in a result which can be applied to exotic
situations such as internal
\infty
-categories in an
\infty
-topos
(
Martini 2022
;
Martini and Wolf 2025
)
.

8.2.
Future work

While this work has constructed the category of categories, much work still remains to be done
around (1) the use of
{\mathsf{Cat}}
in synthetic
\infty
-category theory
and (2) deriving a computational account of this type theory, following on the work of

Weaver and Licata 2020
. For the first point, we believe that higher algebra and the theory of operads
is particularly interesting to study in this regard, as the existing foundations of the
theory
(
Lurie 2017
)
are notoriously technical and rely on intricate simplex-by-simplex
arguments. For the second, we believe that the machinery of our argument could be adapted to
bicubical type theory which would, at the very least, provide a constructive model of our argument.

Appendix A
Formal syntax of
MTT

We provide a succinct description of the formal syntax of
MTT
in this section. Since most
connectives of type theory (
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum}{\textstyle\sum}{\scriptstyle\sum}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum}
,
\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod}{\textstyle\coprod}{\scriptstyle\coprod}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod}
, etc.) are not impacted by modalities, we focus
only on those rules which must be changed. These are (1) some aspects of the substitution calculus
and (2) the rules for modal types are modal
\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod}{\textstyle\prod}{\scriptstyle\prod}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod}
types. We assume a mode theory
\mathcal{M}
which
has 1 object and which is enriched in posets, as is in this paper.

First, we extend contexts with the following new forms:

\frac{\vdash\Gamma\,\mathsf{cx}\quad\mu:m\to m\in\mathcal{M}}{\vdash\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,\mathsf{cx}}\frac{\vdash\Gamma\,\mathsf{cx}\quad\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\vdash A\,\mathsf{type}}{\vdash\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathsf{cx}}

Our previous notation with formal divisions was really syntactic sugar for these operations. In
particular,
x\mathrel{:_{\mu/\nu}}A,y\mathrel{:_{\mathsf{id}/\nu}}B,z:C
becomes

\mathbf{1}.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{id}\mid B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{id}\mid B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{id}\mid B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{id}\mid B$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}.C
. Notably, while
-/\mu
was mere
notation for the paper, it is actually the primitive operation in
MTT
. Any context built using
either notation using the rules of the system is translatable.

We then add several new to the substitution calculus to account for this. This includes a new form
of the variable rule (built using de Bruijn indices) to account for
\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

\frac{\Delta\vdash\gamma:\Gamma}{\Delta.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\vdash\gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}:\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\frac{\vdash\Gamma\,\mathsf{cx}\quad\mu\leq\nu}{\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\vdash\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\leq\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\leq\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\leq\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\leq\nu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}:\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\frac{\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\vdash A\,\mathsf{type}}{\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\mid A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\mid We have normal substitution rules around substitution extensions and weakenings. These are
essentially standard, and so we omit them. We further require a handful of equations which ensure
that
\Gamma\mapsto\Gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
,
\gamma\mapsto\gamma.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
, and
-.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\leq-$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle-\leq-$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\leq-$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\leq-$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

organize into a 2-functor from
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathsf{coop}}
to
\mathbf{Cat}
sending
m
to the category of contexts. We
refer the reader to
Gratzer et al. 2020
for a full account.

{axiom}

[]
There is an equivalence
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{op}\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{op}\mid\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\to\mathbb{I}
which exchanges
0
for
1
and
\land
for

\lor
.

Define a
finitely-presented
\mathbb{I}
-algebra
to be a map of bounded distributive lattice
\mathbb{I}\to X

where
X
is equivalent to a bounded distributive lattice of the form
\mathbb{I}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x_{1}\dots x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x_{1}\dots x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x_{1}\dots x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x_{1}\dots x_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}/\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f_{1}=g_{1}\dots f_{m}=g_{m}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f_{1}=g_{1}\dots f_{m}=g_{m}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f_{1}=g_{1}\dots f_{m}=g_{m}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f_{1}=g_{1}\dots f_{m}=g_{m}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and
\mathbb{I}\to X
is the canonical map. That is,
X
is freely
generated over
\mathbb{I}
by the operations of a bounded distributive lattice, the indeterminates
x_{1}\dots x_{n}
, and subject to the equations
f_{i}=g_{i}
. With this notation to hand, we state a duality
axiom due originally to
Kock 2014
and proposed in this form by
Blechschmidt 2023
.

{axiom}

[]
If
\mathbb{I}\to X
is a finitely presented
\mathbb{I}
-algebra, the following evaluation map is an
equivalence of underlying sets:

\lambda x,f.\,f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}:X\simeq\mathbb{I}^{\hom_{\mathbb{I}/}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X,\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X,\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X,\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X,\mathbb{I}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}
